<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the weak Ma–Trudinger–Wang condition on a fixed smooth connected compact boundaryless Riemannian manifold of dimension at least two implies a common Hölder estimate for optimal transport maps and their inverses over the entire class of probability densities with fixed positive upper and lower bounds. The maps have homeomorphic representatives, conjugate cut points are allowed, and no density regularity is assumed.

- **C001** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Fix $(M,g)$ as above and $0<\lambda\leq\Lambda<\infty$ for which the following density class is nonempty. There are $\alpha\in(0,1]$ and $C<\infty$, depending only on $(M,g),\lambda,\Lambda$, such that the following holds for every pair of measurable densities satisfying $$\int_M\rho_i\,d\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}=1,\qquad \lambda\leq\rho_i\leq\Lambda\quad\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}\text{-almost everywhere}, \qquad i=0,1.$$ The almost-everywhere unique optimal map from $\rho_0\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}$ to $\rho_1\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}$ for the cost $c$ has a homeomorphic representative $\widetilde T:M\to M$, and $$d(\widetilde T(x),\widetilde T(x'))\leq C d(x,x')^\alpha, \qquad d(\widetilde T^{-1}(y),\widetilde T^{-1}(y')) \leq C d(y,y')^\alpha$$ for all $x,x',y,y'\in M$.
  - Explanation: Fixed geometry and positive density bounds give common Hölder control for the transport map and its inverse, with a homeomorphic representative.
- **C002** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geo-prelim)): There is a geometric constant $C_g$ such that the cost is uniformly semiconcave in either variable, including at cut points. Every potential is $D_g$-Lipschitz and uniformly semiconvex. For a continuous representing datum $w$, define $$\mathcal V_u(x)=\{p\in G_x: u(x)+c(x,\exp_xp)+w(\exp_xp)=0\}.$$ Then $\mathcal V_u(x)$ is compact and $\partial u(x)=\operatorname{conv}\mathcal V_u(x)$. Every $p\in\partial u(x)$ has a positive representation using at most $n+1$ affinely independent active logs. The set $\mathcal V_u(x)$ includes every minimizing log to each endpoint active for the specified datum $w$.
  - Explanation: Uniform upper cost supports control potentials and identify ordinary subgradients with convex combinations of active minimizing logs.
- **C003** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geo-radial-index)): Let $p\in G_x$, and let $S_p(r)$ be the Jacobi tensor along $\exp_x(rp)$ with $S_p(0)=0$, $D_rS_p(0)=\mathrm{Id}$, in parallel orthonormal frames. For $0<t<s<1$, $$\frac{d}{dr}H_r(p)=-S_p(r)^{-1}S_p(r)^{-T}, \qquad H_t(p)-H_s(p)\geq\kappa_g(s-t)\mathrm{Id},$$ where $\kappa_g>0$ depends only on the manifold. On every smooth minimizing branch, $A_x(w)w=w$.
  - Explanation: The divided Hessian decreases along minimizing rays, with a uniform positive difference between intermediate times.
- **C004** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geo-slack)): Let $p_i\in G_x$, $m_i>0$, $\sum_i m_i=1$, and set $p=\sum_i m_ip_i$, $E=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{p_i-p\}$. If $t\operatorname{conv}\{p_i\}\subset I(x)$ and $0<t<s<1$, then $$\left[\frac{A_x(tp)}t-\sum_i m_i\frac{A_x(sp_i)}s\right] \bigg\|_{E^\perp}\geq\kappa_g(s-t)\mathrm{Id}.$$ The conclusion also holds at a limiting time $t$ if the radially contracted hulls lie in $I(x)$ and the displayed Hessians have nonconjugate branch limits. No minimizing assumption about $sp$ is needed in this limiting assertion.
  - Explanation: Radial divided-action monotonicity and transverse curvature control give slack on the orthogonal complement of active differences.
- **C005** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geo-short-time)): Let $f$ be Lipschitz and semiconvex. For sufficiently small $t>0$, depending only on these two bounds and the manifold, $$F_t:\Gamma_f\to M,\qquad \Gamma_f=\{(x,p):p\in\partial f(x)\},\qquad F_t(x,p)=\exp_x(tp)$$ is a homeomorphism with locally Lipschitz inverse into the tangent bundle. The minimizing pole $x_t(z)$ of $Q_tf$ is unique, $Q_tf\in C^{1,1}$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}Q_tf(z)=-t^{-1}\log_zx_t(z)$. The graph $\Gamma_f$ is an $n$-dimensional Lipschitz manifold.
  - Explanation: Short-time regularization of a Lipschitz semiconvex datum gives a unique pole and a locally Lipschitz inverse projection.
- **C006** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-support)): For every potential $u$, every $x\in M$, and every $p\in\partial u(x)$, one has $p\in G_x$ and $$u(x')\geq u(x)+c(x,\exp_xp)-c(x',\exp_xp) \qquad(x'\in M).$$ For each $0<t<1$, $F_t:\Gamma_u\to M$ is a homeomorphism, $tp\in I(x)$, and its poles are the unique global minimizers of $Q_tu$.
  - Explanation: Every ordinary subgradient of a potential generates a global supporting cost function and a unique intermediate pole.
- **C007** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geo-convexity)): Both $G_x$ and $I(x)$ are convex for every $x\in M$.
  - Explanation: The open and closed injectivity domains are convex.
- **C008** (bound, [source](#stmt-geo-sections)): For a dual pair $(u,v)$, each set $$\mathcal S_x(r)=\left\{p\in G_x: u(x)+\frac{\|p\|^2}{2}+v(\exp_xp)\leq r\right\}$$ is compact and convex, including ordinary and conjugate cut endpoints. If it is nonempty and $r\geq0$, then $$\operatorname{diam}\mathcal S_x(r)^2 \leq8\left(r+\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{p\in\mathcal S_x(r)}v(\exp_xp)\right).$$
  - Explanation: Lifted gap sections remain compact and convex at cut endpoints, and their diameters are controlled by gap height and potential oscillation.
- **C009** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geo-intermediate)): Let $(u,v)$ be a dual pair and $0<t<1$. Then $Q_tu=-Q_{1-t}v\in C^{1,1}(M)$, with almost-everywhere bounds $$-\frac{C_g}{1-t}g\leq\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}Q_tu\leq\frac{C_g}{t}g.$$ If $(x_t(z),p_t(z))=F_t^{-1}(z)$ and $\Phi_s$ is geodesic flow on $TM$, then $$(x_t(z),p_t(z))=\Phi_{-t}(z,\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}Q_tu(z)).$$ In particular, the pole and momentum maps are Lipschitz with constants depending only on $M,g,t$. There are also $r_t,b_t>0$, depending only on $M,g,t$, such that every $z=\exp_x(tp)$, $p\in\partial u(x)$, satisfies $$u(x')+c_t(x',z)\geq u(x)+c_t(x,z)+b_t d(x,x')^2 \qquad\text{if }d(x,x')<r_t.$$ All these constants are uniform when $t$ ranges in a compact subinterval of $(0,1)$.
  - Explanation: Intermediate dual regularizations have uniform Hessian bounds and Lipschitz pole and momentum maps.
- **C010** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-spec-identities)): Let $\gamma(s)=\exp_x(sv)$ be a geodesic and identify its tangent spaces by parallel transport. Put $\mathcal R(s)w=R(w,\dot\gamma(s))\dot\gamma(s)$ and let $C,J$ solve $$U''+\mathcal R U=0, \qquad C(0)=\mathrm{Id},\quad C'(0)=0,\quad J(0)=0,\quad J'(0)=\mathrm{Id}.$$ Then $$\label{spec:exp-jacobi} (d\exp_x)_{Tv}=\frac{J(T)}{T}$$ for $T>0$. Whenever the geodesic to $\gamma(T)$ is nonconjugate, let $c_{\gamma,T}$ be its smooth local action: one half the squared length of the geodesic branch obtained by varying the two endpoints. The source Hessian of its divided action is $$\label{spec:divided-hessian} S(T):=D^2_{xx}\frac{c_{\gamma,T}(x,\gamma(T))}{T} =J(T)^{-1}C(T), \qquad S'(T)=-J(T)^{-1}J(T)^{-*}.$$ The endpoint is held fixed when taking $D^2_{xx}$; the derivative in $T$ then follows the displayed geodesic. The identities apply to a smooth action branch whether or not it minimizes. Before cut, this action is the cost $c$; at a nonconjugate minimizing cut vector, it is the chosen smooth upper branch of $c$.* *If $y=\exp_xv$ and $\bar v=-P_{x\to y}v$ is the reverse initial velocity, then $$\label{spec:reversal} (d\exp_y)_{\bar v}=\bigl((d\exp_x)_v\bigr)^*, \qquad \sigma_y(\bar v)=\sigma_x(v),$$ with the indicated source and target tangent spaces. Finally, for $v\in I(x)$, $$\label{spec:radial-identity} A_x(v)v=v.$$
  - Explanation: Jacobi evolution determines exponential differentials, divided Hessians, reversal, and the radial identity, with the order of inverse factors retained.
- **C011** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-radial)): There are constants $c_{\rm r}>0$ and $C_{\rm r}<\infty$, depending only on $(M,g)$, such that for $x\in M$, $v\in G_x$, $0<\delta\le1/2$, and $1\le j\le n$, $$\label{spec:singular-shortening} c_{\rm r}\bigl(s_j(x,v)+\delta\bigr) \le s_j(x,(1-\delta)v) \le C_{\rm r}\bigl(s_j(x,v)+\delta\bigr).$$ There is also a positive definite form $Q_{x,v,\delta}$ on $T_xM$ such that, on putting $$N_{x,\delta}(v) =-\delta\bigl(A_x((1-\delta)v)-\mathrm{Id}\bigr),$$ one has $$\begin{aligned} &\\|N_{x,\delta}(v)-Q_{x,v,\delta}\\|\le C_{\rm r}\delta, \label{spec:bounded-background}\\ &c_{\rm r}\frac{\delta}{s_j(x,v)+\delta} \le\lambda_j^{\downarrow}(Q_{x,v,\delta}) \le C_{\rm r}\frac{\delta}{s_j(x,v)+\delta}. \label{spec:hessian-spectrum} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, uniformly on this set, $$\label{spec:bounded-N} \\|N_{x,\delta}(v)\\|\le C_{\rm r},\qquad N_{x,\delta}(v)\succeq-C_{\rm r}\delta\mathrm{Id}, \qquad N_{x,\delta}(v)v=0.$$ No condition on the multiplicity of conjugacy is required.
  - Explanation: Radial shortening controls ordered singular values and a positive Hessian comparison form even at conjugate endpoints.
- **C012** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-radial-determinants)): With a constant depending only on $(M,g)$, if $v\in G_x$ and $1/2\le\theta\le1$, then $$\label{spec:radial-det} \sigma_x(\theta v)\ge c\sigma_x(v).$$ Suppose $y=\exp_xp$, $p\in G_x$, and $\bar p=-P_{x\to y}p$. If $l\in[1/2,1)$ and $\bar p/l\in G_y$, then $$\label{spec:center-true-det} \sigma_y(\bar p/l)\le C\sigma_x(p).$$ If $l_+\in[1,2]$, then $$\label{spec:outer-true-det} \sigma_y(\bar p/l_+)\ge c\sigma_x(p).$$ Comparison <a href="#spec:center-true-det" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:center-true-det">[spec:center-true-det]</a> permits the longer reversed vector $\bar p/l$ to have a conjugate endpoint.
  - Explanation: Radial shortening and reversal compare exponential determinants for the center and outer rays in their respective directions.
- **C013** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-pullback)): There is $c_{\rm p}>0$, depending only on $(M,g)$, with the following property. Let $y\in M$, $r\in T_yM$, and $1<T_*\le2$ satisfy $T_*r\in G_y$. Put $$\gamma(s)=\exp_y(sr),\quad x=\gamma(1),\quad p=-P_{y\to x}r,\quad L=(d\exp_x)_p, \qquad \tau=1+\frac{T_*-1}{2}.$$ Then all costs appearing in $$\label{spec:half-extension-gain} L^*\left[A_y(r)-\frac1\tau A_y(\tau r)\right]L \succeq c_{\rm p}(T_*-1)\mathrm{Id}$$ are smooth. If the terminal ray at time $T_*$ is nonconjugate, the same inequality holds with $\tau$ replaced by $T_*$ and its chosen smooth branch. In particular, for $T_*=1/l$ this latter inequality reads $$\label{spec:full-extension-gain} L^*\left[A_y(r)-l A_y(r/l)\right]L \succeq c_{\rm p}(1-l)\mathrm{Id}, \qquad 1/2\le l<1.$$ Without any uniformity assertion, the source Hessian of divided action decreases strictly in the positive definite order between any two nonconjugate times on a minimizing ray.
  - Explanation: An extra minimizing extension gives an isotropic gain after pullback, without differentiating a Hessian at a conjugate terminal point.
- **C014** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-comparison)): Assume weak MTW. Fix $a_0>0$ and $B<\infty$. There is $c_{\rm s}>0$, depending only on $(M,g),a_0,B$, such that the following holds. Let $x\in M$, $p,q\in T_xM$, $q\ne0$, satisfy $$\label{spec:segment-hypotheses} p+tq\in G_x\quad(-a_0\le t\le1),\qquad p\cdot q>0,\qquad \frac{\|q\|^2}{p\cdot q}\le B.$$ Then, for every $1\le j\le n$, $$\label{spec:ordered-comparison} s_j(x,p)\ge c_{\rm s}s_j(x,p+q).$$ In particular, $$\label{spec:product-comparison} \sigma_x(p)\ge c_{\rm s}^n\sigma_x(p+q).$$
  - Explanation: Weak curvature control compares ordered singular values along an extended affine segment with controlled outward geometry.
- **C015** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-outward)): Assume weak MTW and the convexity of $G_x$ established in Corollary <a href="#geo:convexity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:convexity">7</a>. Fix $\mu\in(0,1)$ and positive constants $\kappa,L_0$. Suppose $$p=\sum_{i=1}^N m_i p_i,\qquad p_i\in G_x,\quad m_i>0,\quad\sum_i m_i=1,$$ and for one index $i$ there are $D>0$, a unit vector $e$, and $t_i>0$ such that $$\label{spec:outward-data} p_i=p+t_i e,\quad h:=p\cdot e>0,\quad h^2\ge\kappa D,\quad \|p_i\|^2-\|p\|^2\le L_0D,\quad m_i\ge\mu.$$ Then $$\label{spec:outward-det} \sigma_x(p)\ge c\sigma_x(p_i),$$ where $c>0$ depends only on $(M,g),\mu,\kappa,L_0$.
  - Explanation: A positive vertex weight supplies an opposite extension, allowing the affine comparison to control the selected outward Jacobian.
- **C016** (bound, [source](#stmt-sc-power)): If there are $\beta>0$, $C_0<\infty$, and $r_0>0$ such that $F(r)\leq C_0r^\beta$ for $0<r<r_0$, then all the optimal maps in the fixed density class have homeomorphic representatives with a common Hölder estimate in both directions. One may take any exponent no larger than $\frac12\min\{\beta,1\}$.
  - Explanation: A common power bound for section oscillations gives singleton contacts and a common Hölder estimate in both directions.
- **C017** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sc-plateau)): Suppose that $F$ has no power upper bound near zero. There are $r_j<R_j\to0$, pairs $(u_j,v_j)\in\mathcal C$, poles $x_j$, and numbers $a_j,D_j$ with $D_j>0$ such that $$\begin{gathered} \min_{S_{r_j}(x_j;u_j,v_j)}v_j=a_j,\qquad \max_{S_{r_j}(x_j;u_j,v_j)}v_j=a_j+D_j, \label{sc:endlevels}\\ \frac{r_j}{R_j}\longrightarrow0,\qquad F(R_j)\leq(1+\delta_j)D_j,\qquad \delta_j\longrightarrow0,\qquad \frac{\|\log D_j\|}{\|\log R_j\|}\longrightarrow0. \label{sc:plateau-data} \end{gathered}$$ Consequently $R_j/D_j^k\to0$ for every fixed $k>0$.
  - Explanation: Failure of every power bound selects separated scales whose class-wide oscillations are almost equal.
- **C018** (construction, [source](#stmt-sc-templates)): There is a fixed smooth function $B_c:\mathbb R\to(-1,1)$ with bounded derivatives such that $$B_c'<0\text{ on }\mathbb R,\qquad B_c(s)\geq1-\varepsilon_0\quad(s\leq1/2),\qquad B_c(s)<0\quad(s\geq3/4).$$ Given a cap $M_0\geq2$ and $K\geq1$, there are $0<\eta\leq1/128$ and a smooth bounded function $B_o\geq0$, with bounded derivatives, satisfying $$\begin{aligned} &B_o(s)\geq1\quad(s\leq1-\eta),\qquad B_o(1)=0,\qquad 1\leq B_o(0)\leq1+\varepsilon_0, \label{sc:outer-values}\\ &B_o(s)\geq B_o(0)+1 \quad(1/16\leq s\leq1-\eta), \label{sc:barrier}\\ &B_o'(s)>0,\quad B_o''(s)\leq-K \quad\text{if }-2\eta\leq s\leq1-\eta \text{ and }B_o(s)\leq M_0. \label{sc:curvature} \end{aligned}$$ One can arrange $\\|B_o\\|_\infty\leq2M_0+8$, independently of $K$, and make $\eta$ arbitrarily smaller if desired.
  - Explanation: Smooth scalar templates provide a decreasing center profile and an outer barrier with prescribed concavity under a fixed value cap.
- **C019** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sc-switch)): For all sufficiently small selected scales, uniformly for $b\in[b_-,b_+]$, there is $$\frac b{32D}\leq\tau_b\leq\frac{8b}D \label{sc:switch-time}$$ such that, at $x_b=\exp_{x_0}(\tau_b p_0)$, the function $g_b$ has active endpoints $y_-$ and $y_+$ with $$-2\eta\leq s_{y_-}<1/16,\qquad s_{y_+}\geq1-\eta.$$ The original endpoints $y_0,y_1$ and every active endpoint at this pole lie in one original section $S_{C b}(x_b;u,v)$, where $C$ depends only on the geometry and $H_o$. In particular every active level is in $[-\delta_j,1+\delta_j]$. For every minimizing log $q_i$ to an active endpoint, $$\|q_i\|^2=P-2D s_{y_i}+O_{H_o}(\tau_b+b) =P-2D s_{y_i}+o(D). \label{sc:switch-norms}$$ The constants in <a href="#sc:switch-time" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sc:switch-time">[sc:switch-time]</a> are independent of $K$ and of the height and derivatives of the outer template.
  - Explanation: Moving the pole toward the low endpoint forces simultaneous low and high active endpoints while excluding middle levels.
- **C020** (bound, [source](#stmt-sc-excess)): For all sufficiently small selected scales and all $b\in[b_-,b_+]$, $$c_0b\leq E(b)\leq b, \label{sc:excess-bounds}$$ with the constant in <a href="#sc:numerical" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sc:numerical">[sc:numerical]</a>, independently of $K$.
  - Explanation: The center modification exceeds the double transform of the outer modification by a positive amount proportional to amplitude.
- **C021** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sc-maximum)): Fix a sufficiently small selected scale. For every $t<1$ sufficiently close to one, the continuous function $$\Phi_t(b,z)=Q_tg_b(z)+Q_{1-t}w_{c,b}(z)-\pi(b),\qquad (b,z)\in[b_-,b_+]\times M,$$ has a positive global maximum with $b$ in the interior of its interval. Both spatial terms are $C^{1,1}$, and both minimizing points are unique. If $x$ and $y$ are these minimizing points at a maximum, there is $p\in\partial g_b(x)\cap G_x$ such that $$z=\exp_x(tp),\qquad y=\exp_xp,\qquad Q_tg_b(z)+Q_{1-t}w_{c,b}(z)=E_b(y)>0. \label{sc:aligned}$$ Moreover $(x,y)$ is before cut.
  - Explanation: A penalty and intermediate regularization produce a positive interior maximum with aligned minimizing rays.
- **C022** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sc-parameter)): At any maximum in Lemma <a href="#sc:maximum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sc:maximum">22</a>, let $$p=\sum_{i=1}^N m_i p_i,\qquad m_i>0,\qquad\sum_i m_i=1,$$ be any finite representation by minimizing logs active for the displayed representation $g_b=w_{o,b}^c$ at its minimizing pole $x$. Thus $g_b(x)+c(x,\exp_xp_i)+w_{o,b}(\exp_xp_i)=0$. Write $y_i=\exp_xp_i$ and $s_i=(v(y_i)-a)/D$, and let $s=(v(y)-a)/D$ be the center level. Then $$B_c(s)-\sum_i m_iB_o(s_i)\geq \pi'(b)\geq c_1. \label{sc:parameter-inequality}$$
  - Explanation: One-sided variation of amplitude gives a template inequality for every positive finite active representation.
- **C023** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sc-levels)): At a maximum in Lemma <a href="#sc:maximum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sc:maximum">22</a>, the center and all active endpoints belong to $S_{(H_o+2)b}(x;u,v)$. For every active log $p_i$, $$v(y_i)-v(y)=\frac{\|p\|^2-\|p_i\|^2}{2} +b\{B_c(s)-B_o(s_i)\}-E_b(y). \label{sc:level-identity}$$ In every positive barycentric representation the total weight on levels $s_i\geq1-\eta$ is at least $c_1$. Moreover $$s<3/4,\qquad \max\{s,s_i\}-\min\{s,s_i\}\leq1+\delta_j,\qquad \min\{s,s_i\}\geq-2\eta \label{sc:level-bounds}$$ for small scales. These conclusions hold at each exact maximum, before taking a prefix-time limit.
  - Explanation: The center and active endpoints share a controlled original section, and every positive representation carries positive weight on high levels.
- **C024** (bound, [source](#stmt-sc-outward)): Suppose $p=\sum_{i=1}^N m_ip_i$ in an $n$-dimensional Euclidean space, with $N\leq n+1$, $m_i>0$, and $\sum_i m_i=1$. Suppose $E=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{p_i-p\}=\mathbb Re$, where $\|e\|=1$, and $q=p\cdot e>0$. Assume the template and level inequalities <a href="#sc:parameter-inequality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sc:parameter-inequality">[sc:parameter-inequality]</a>, <a href="#sc:level-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sc:level-bounds">[sc:level-bounds]</a>, and $$\left\|\ \|p_i\|^2-\|p\|^2+2D(s_i-s)\ \right\|\leq\zeta D,\qquad 0\leq\delta_j\leq\zeta\leq1/16.$$ There is an outward index $i$ with $p_i=p+d_i e$, $d_i>0$, such that $$\begin{gathered} m_i\geq m_*:=\frac{c_1}{12(n+1)},\qquad m_id_i\geq\frac{c_1D}{8(n+1)q},\qquad d_i\leq\frac{3D}{2q}, \label{sc:outward-weight}\\ 0<\|p_i\|^2-\|p\|^2\leq3D, \label{sc:outward-norm}\\ -2\eta\leq s_i<1-\eta,\qquad B_o(s_i)\leq1/m_*. \label{sc:outward-cap} \end{gathered}$$ In particular the cap $M_0=2/m_*$ can be fixed before $K$. All these constants are independent of $K$.
  - Explanation: Collinear barycentric balance selects an outward endpoint with controlled weight, moment, norm increment, and template value.
- **C025** (bound, [source](#stmt-jet-contact-volume)): Let $u,v$ be the dual potentials for an admissible pair of densities, and let $$\mathcal C=\{(x,y):u(x)+c(x,y)+v(y)=0\}.$$ For a Borel set $A$ on either side, its contact image satisfies $$\label{jet:contact-volume-eq} \frac{\lambda}{\Lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(A) \le \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(\mathcal C(A)) \le \frac{\Lambda}{\lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(A).$$ Projections are interpreted in the completed volume measures.
  - Explanation: The original positive density bounds control contact-image volume on either side in the completed measures.
- **C026** (bound, [source](#stmt-jet-viscosity)): Let $v$ be one of the original dual potentials. Suppose a $C^2$ function $\phi$ touches $v$ from below at $y$, its gradient $r=\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}\phi(y)$ belongs to $I(y)$, and $$W=\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}\phi(y)+A_y(r)>0.$$ Then $$\label{jet:viscosity-eq} \sigma_y(r)\det W\le \Lambda/\lambda.$$ The same conclusion holds for a lower Taylor jet with these properties, by approximation of its quadratic part.
  - Explanation: A lower test with before-cut gradient and positive relative Hessian satisfies an upper determinant bound from original contact volume.
- **C027** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-jet-transfer)): Suppose $z_0$ is a point where $F$ has a second-order expansion and $x(z)$ is differentiable. Write $x=x(z_0)$, $z_0=\exp_x(tp)$, and choose active logs $$p=\sum_{j=1}^Nm_jp_j,\qquad m_j>0,\quad \sum_jm_j=1,\qquad N\le n+1,$$ where $y_j=\exp_xp_j$ and $g(x)+c(x,y_j)+w_o(y_j)=0$. Set $E=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{p_j-p:1\le j\le N\}$. In the fixed-pole coordinates $z(q)=\exp_x(tq)$, define $$\label{jet:L-definition} L=D_q^2\{c_t(x,z(q))-F(z(q))\}\big\|_{q=p}.$$ Then $$\label{jet:L-properties} L\ge0,\qquad LE=0.$$ For every $j$, the function $$\Psi_j(d)=w_o(\exp_x(p_j+d))+\tfrac12\|p_j+d\|^2$$ has at $d=0$ the lower Taylor jet $(0,m_jL)$, after subtracting its value at zero. If $p_j$ is nonconjugate, this is an endpoint lower test in the smooth chosen cost branch.* *More precisely, for $t<s<1$ put $$Q_s=\frac1tA_x(tp)-\frac1s\sum_jm_j A_x(sp_j).$$ Then $Q_s\ge0$ on $E^\perp$, and $m_j(L+LQ_sL)$ is a lower Taylor Hessian for $\Psi_j$.
  - Explanation: The exact pole matrix is positive semidefinite, annihilates active differences, and transfers lower jets to active endpoints.
- **C028** (bound, [source](#stmt-jet-semibound)): Fix one set of scales, one original potential $v$, and the two templates. Let $t_j\uparrow1$ and $b_j\in[b_-,b_+]$. Put $$F_j=Q_{t_j}g_{b_j},\qquad G_j=Q_{1-t_j}w_{c,b_j}.$$ Let $z_j$ be simultaneous differentiability points for the objects in Lemma <a href="#jet:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="jet:transfer">28</a>, with second-order expansions of $G_j$, whose center levels lie in the fixed compact interval above. Suppose $$\|\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}(F_j+G_j)(z_j)\|\longrightarrow0.$$ Let $x_j$ be the prefix pole and $z_j=\exp_{x_j}(t_jp_j)$. The matrix $H_j$ of <a href="#jet:H-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="jet:H-definition">[jet:H-definition]</a> satisfies $$\label{jet:semibound-eq} H_j\ge c\frac{b_j}{D}\mathrm{Id} -C\frac{b_j}{D^2}p_jp_j^*-\varepsilon_j\mathrm{Id}, \qquad \varepsilon_j\longrightarrow0.$$ The constants $c,C$ depend on the geometry and the fixed center template, but not on $K$. The errors tend to zero with the prefix and gradient errors at the fixed scales. No upper bound on $H_j$, and no Alexandrov Hessian of $v$ at the center, is assumed.
  - Explanation: A smooth split support bounds the center matrix from below before using a center density equation.
- **C029** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-jet-outer-conjugacy)): Fix $b>0$ and an active outer pair $$g_b(x)+c(x,y)+\varphi_o(v(y))=0.$$ Choose any minimizing reverse log $r$ from $y$ to $x$. Suppose $$l=\varphi_o'(v(y))>1,\qquad \varphi_o''(v(y))<0.$$ Then the chosen modified ray is nonconjugate at its endpoint. The true log $r/l$ is before cut.
  - Explanation: An active increasing concave outer modification excludes conjugacy by contradicting the original lower-test determinant bound.
- **C030** (bound, [source](#stmt-jet-outer-test)): At a pair as in Lemma <a href="#jet:outer-conjugacy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="jet:outer-conjugacy">30</a>, let $p=\log_xy$ be the corresponding forward log, $B=(d\exp_x)_p$, and let $H_o\ge0$ be a lower Taylor Hessian, with zero linear part, of $d\mapsto w_o(\exp_x(p+d))+\tfrac12\|p+d\|^2$. Define $$\begin{aligned} V_o={}&H_o+B^*\{lA_y(r/l)-A_y(r)\}B -\frac{\varphi_o''(v(y))}{l^2}pp^* . \label{jet:outer-form} \end{aligned}$$ Then $V_o>0$ and $$\label{jet:outer-det} \det V_o\le l^n(\Lambda/\lambda)\, \frac{\sigma_x(p)^2}{\sigma_y(r/l)}.$$ The middle term in <a href="#jet:outer-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="jet:outer-form">[jet:outer-form]</a> is positive definite. On a compact family of active pairs where $b>0$ is bounded away from zero, $\varphi_o'>1$ and $\varphi_o''<0$ hold with fixed strict margins, this term has a uniform positive definite baseline and the chosen exponential branches are uniformly nonsingular.
  - Explanation: Undoing the outer scalar modification yields a positive relative form and an upper determinant estimate on the selected branch.
- **C031** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-jet-subgradient-differential)): If $f$ is semiconvex near $0$ and has the Alexandrov expansion $$f(h)=f(0)+p\cdot h+\tfrac12 A(h,h)+o(\|h\|^2),$$ then every nearby subgradient satisfies $$\zeta=p+Ah+o(\|h\|),\qquad \zeta\in\partial f(h),$$ uniformly over the choice of $\zeta$.
  - Explanation: An Alexandrov expansion controls every neighboring subgradient uniformly, allowing differentiation through nonsmooth neighboring endpoints.
- **C032** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-jet-determinants)): Fix the center template and the constants of Lemmas <a href="#sc:excess" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sc:excess">21</a>, <a href="#sc:maximum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sc:maximum">22</a>, <a href="#sc:parameter" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sc:parameter">23</a>, and <a href="#sc:outward" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sc:outward">25</a>. Choose $K$, then an outer template with curvature less than $-K$ in the prescribed capped region. For all sufficiently small scales in the sequence of Lemma <a href="#sc:plateau" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sc:plateau">18</a>, there are a parameter $b\in[b_-,b_+]$, a pole $x$, center and active logs $p,p_i\in G_x$, weights $m_i>0$ summing to one, and symmetric matrices $H,L,V,V_i$ on $T_xM$, with the following properties. For one selected index $i$, $$\begin{gathered} p=\sum_jm_jp_j,\qquad E=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{p_j-p\}=\mathbb Re,\qquad \|e\|=1,\quad q=p\cdot e>0,\quad q^2\ge cD, \label{jet:limiting-geometry}\\ m_i\ge m_*>0,\qquad p_i=p+d_ie,\quad d_i>0, \label{jet:limiting-outward}\\ L\ge0,\qquad LE=0,\qquad H\le L,\qquad H\ge V+c\frac bD\mathrm{Id}-C\frac b{D^2}pp^*,\qquad V>0, \label{jet:limiting-center}\\ V_i\ge m_iL+cK\frac b{D^2}p_ip_i^*,\qquad V_i>0. \label{jet:limiting-outer} \end{gathered}$$ The center modified log and the selected outer modified log are nonconjugate. Let $r$ and $r_i$ denote the reverse logs of these specified rays, let $y=\exp_xp$, $y_i=\exp_xp_i$, and set $l=\varphi_c'(v(y))$, $l_i=\varphi_o'(v(y_i))$. The chosen true limiting branches are also nonconjugate, and $$\label{jet:limiting-determinants} \det V\ge c\,\frac{\sigma_x(p)^2}{\sigma_y(r/l)}, \qquad \det V_i\le C\,\frac{\sigma_x(p_i)^2}{\sigma_{y_i}(r_i/l_i)}.$$ Here $c,C,m_*$ are independent of $K$ and the outer template. The level and norm-increment conclusions of Lemma <a href="#sc:outward" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sc:outward">25</a> hold as well.
  - Explanation: Ordered limiting and sampling arguments produce positive center and outer forms after the outer test bounds the center Hessian.
- **C033** (bound, [source](#stmt-jet-matrix)): Suppose the matrices and vectors satisfy <a href="#jet:limiting-geometry" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="jet:limiting-geometry">[jet:limiting-geometry]</a>–<a href="#jet:limiting-outer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="jet:limiting-outer">[jet:limiting-outer]</a>. Then $$\label{jet:matrix-gain} \det V_i\ge cK\det V,$$ where $c>0$ depends on the fixed center-template constants, the dimension, and $m_*$, and is independent of $K$.
  - Explanation: Oblique projection isolates the missing direction and gives a determinant gain proportional to the outer curvature parameter.
- **C034** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-jet-contradiction)): The section-oscillation function of Lemma <a href="#sc:plateau" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sc:plateau">18</a> has a positive power upper bound at zero.
  - Explanation: The determinant gain and geometric Jacobian comparisons contradict arbitrarily large outer curvature, forcing a positive power bound.

For a continuous representing datum $w$, define $$\\mathcal V_u(x)=\\{p\\in G_x:\n              u(x)+c(x,\\exp_xp)+w(\\exp_xp)=0\\}.$$ Then $\\mathcal V_u(x)$ is compact and $\\partial u(x)=\\operatorname{conv}\\mathcal V_u(x)$. Every $p\\in\\partial u(x)$ has a positive representation using at most $n+1$ affinely independent active logs. The set $\\mathcal V_u(x)$ includes every minimizing log to each endpoint active for the specified datum $w$.",
          "source_id": "PM-360.2"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-geo-short-time",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be Lipschitz and semiconvex.         "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-intermediate",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(u,v)$ be a dual pair and $0<t<1$. Then $Q_tu=-Q_{1-t}v\\in C^{1,1}(M)$, with almost-everywhere bounds $$-\\frac{C_g}{1-t}g\\leq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}Q_tu\\leq\\frac{C_g}{t}g.$$ If $(x_t(z),p_t(z))=F_t^{-1}(z)$ and $\\Phi_s$ is geodesic flow on $TM$, then $$(x_t(z),p_t(z))=\\Phi_{-t}(z,\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}Q_tu(z)).$$ In particular, the pole and momentum maps are Lipschitz with constants depending only on $M,g,t$. There are also $r_t,b_t>0$, depending only on $M,g,t$, such that every $z=\\exp_x(tp)$, $p\\in\\partial u(x)$, satisfies $$u(x')+c_t(x',z)\\geq\n u(x)+c_t(x,z)+b_t d(x,x')^2\n \\qquad\\text{if }d(x,x')<r_t.$$ All these constants are uniform when $t$ ranges in a compact subinterval of $(0,1)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },

        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-identities",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma(s)=\\exp_x(sv)$ be a geodesic and identify its tangent spaces by parallel transport. Put $\\mathcal R(s)w=R(w,\\dot\\gamma(s))\\dot\\gamma(s)$ and let $C,J$ solve $$U''+\\mathcal R U=0,\n  \\qquad C(0)=\\mathrm{Id},\\quad C'(0)=0,\\quad\n  J(0)=0,\\quad J'(0)=\\mathrm{Id}.$$ Then $$\\label{spec:exp-jacobi}\n  (d\\exp_x)_{Tv}=\\frac{J(T)}{T}$$ for $T>0$. Whenever the geodesic to $\\gamma(T)$ is nonconjugate, let $c_{\\gamma,T}$ be its smooth local action: one half the squared length of the geodesic branch obtained by varying the two endpoints. The source Hessian of its divided action is $$\\label{spec:divided-hessian}\n  S(T):=D^2_{xx}\\frac{c_{\\gamma,T}(x,\\gamma(T))}{T}\n       =J(T)^{-1}C(T),\n  \\qquad\n  S'(T)=-J(T)^{-1}J(T)^{-*}.$$ The endpoint is held fixed when taking $D^2_{xx}$; the derivative in $T$ then follows the displayed geodesic. The identities apply to a smooth action branch whether or not it minimizes. Before cut, this action is the cost $c$; at a nonconjugate minimizing cut vector, it is the chosen smooth upper branch of $c$.*\n\n\n\n*If $y=\\exp_xv$ and $\\bar v=-P_{x\\to y}v$ is the reverse initial velocity, then $$\\label{spec:reversal}\n  (d\\exp_y)_{\\bar v}=\\bigl((d\\exp_x)_v\\bigr)^*,\n  \\qquad \\sigma_y(\\bar v)=\\sigma_x(v),$$ with the indicated source and target tangent spaces. Finally, for $v\\in I(x)$, $$\\label{spec:radial-identity}\n  A_x(v)v=v.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-radial",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $c_{\\rm r}>0$ and $C_{\\rm r}<\\infty$, depending only on $(M,g)$, such that for $x\\in M$, $v\\in G_x$, $0<\\delta\\le1/2$, and $1\\le j\\le n$, $$\\label{spec:singular-shortening}\n  c_{\\rm r}\\bigl(s_j(x,v)+\\delta\\bigr)\n   \\le s_j(x,(1-\\delta)v)\n   \\le C_{\\rm r}\\bigl(s_j(x,v)+\\delta\\bigr).$$ There is also a positive definite form $Q_{x,v,\\delta}$ on $T_xM$ such that, on putting $$N_{x,\\delta}(v)\n       =-\\delta\\bigl(A_x((1-\\delta)v)-\\mathrm{Id}\\bigr),$$ one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n  &\\|N_{x,\\delta}(v)-Q_{x,v,\\delta}\\|\\le C_{\\rm r}\\delta,\n       \\label{spec:bounded-background}\\\\\n  &c_{\\rm r}\\frac{\\delta}{s_j(x,v)+\\delta}\n     \\le\\lambda_j^{\\downarrow}(Q_{x,v,\\delta})\n     \\le C_{\\rm r}\\frac{\\delta}{s_j(x,v)+\\delta}.\n       \\label{spec:hessian-spectrum}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, uniformly on this set, $$\\label{spec:bounded-N}\n  \\|N_{x,\\delta}(v)\\|\\le C_{\\rm r},\\qquad\n  N_{x,\\delta}(v)\\succeq-C_{\\rm r}\\delta\\mathrm{Id},\n  \\qquad N_{x,\\delta}(v)v=0.$$ No condition on the multiplicity of conjugacy is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-radial",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $c_{\\rm r}>0$ and $C_{\\rm r}<\\infty$, depending only on $(M,g)$, such that for $x\\in M$, $v\\in G_x$, $0<\\delta\\le1/2$, and $1\\le j\\le n$, $$\\label{spec:singular-shortening}\n  c_{\\rm r}\\bigl(s_j(x,v)+\\delta\\bigr)\n   \\le s_j(x,(1-\\delta)v)\n   \\le C_{\\rm r}\\bigl(s_j(x,v)+\\delta\\bigr).$$ There is also a positive definite form $Q_{x,v,\\delta}$ on $T_xM$ such that, on putting $$N_{x,\\delta}(v)\n       =-\\delta\\bigl(A_x((1-\\delta)v)-\\mathrm{Id}\\bigr),$$ one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n  &\\|N_{x,\\delta}(v)-Q_{x,v,\\delta}\\|\\le C_{\\rm r}\\delta,\n       \\label{spec:bounded-background}\\\\\n  &c_{\\rm r}\\frac{\\delta}{s_j(x,v)+\\delta}\n     \\le\\lambda_j^{\\downarrow}(Q_{x,v,\\delta})\n     \\le C_{\\rm r}\\frac{\\delta}{s_j(x,v)+\\delta}.\n       \\label{spec:hessian-spectrum}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, uniformly on this set, $$\\label{spec:bounded-N}\n  \\|N_{x,\\delta}(v)\\|\\le C_{\\rm r},\\qquad\n  N_{x,\\delta}(v)\\succeq-C_{\\rm r}\\delta\\mathrm{Id},\n  \\qquad N_{x,\\delta}(v)v=0.$$ No condition on the multiplicity of conjugacy is required.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-radial-determinants",
        "exact_quote": "With a constant depending only on $(M,g)$, if $v\\in G_x$ and $1/2\\le\\theta\\le1$, then $$\\label{spec:radial-det}\n  \\sigma_x(\\theta v)\\ge c\\sigma_x(v).$$ Suppose $y=\\exp_xp$, $p\\in G_x$, and $\\bar p=-P_{x\\to y}p$. If $l\\in[1/2,1)$ and $\\bar p/l\\in G_y$, then $$\\label{spec:center-true-det}\n  \\sigma_y(\\bar p/l)\\le C\\sigma_x(p).$$ If $l_+\\in[1,2]$, then $$\\label{spec:outer-true-det}\n  \\sigma_y(\\bar p/l_+)\\ge c\\sigma_x(p).$$ Comparison <a href=\"#spec:center-true-det\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:center-true-det\">[spec:center-true-det]</a> permits the longer reversed vector $\\bar p/l$ to have a conjugate endpoint.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-radial-determinants",
          "exact_quote": "With a constant depending only on $(M,g)$, if $v\\in G_x$ and $1/2\\le\\theta\\le1$, then $$\\label{spec:radial-det}\n  \\sigma_x(\\theta v)\\ge c\\sigma_x(v).$$ Suppose $y=\\exp_xp$, $p\\in G_x$, and $\\bar p=-P_{x\\to y}p$. If $l\\in[1/2,1)$ and $\\bar p/l\\in G_y$, then $$\\label{spec:center-true-det}\n  \\sigma_y(\\bar p/l)\\le C\\sigma_x(p).$$ If $l_+\\in[1,2]$, then $$\\label{spec:outer-true-det}\n  \\sigma_y(\\bar p/l_+)\\ge c\\sigma_x(p).$$ Comparison <a href=\"#spec:center-true-det\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:center-true-det\">[spec:center-true-det]</a> permits the longer reversed vector $\\bar p/l$ to have a conjugate endpoint.",
          "source_id": "PM-360.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "With a constant depending only on $(M,g)$, if $v\\in G_x$ and $1/2\\le\\theta\\le1$, then $$\\label{spec:radial-det}\n  \\sigma_x(\\theta v)\\ge c\\sigma_x(v).$$ Suppose $y=\\exp_xp$, $p\\in G_x$, and $\\bar p=-P_{x\\to y}p$. If $l\\in[1/2,1)$ and $\\bar p/l\\in G_y$, then $$\\label{spec:center-true-det}\n  \\sigma_y(\\bar p/l)\\le C\\sigma_x(p).$$ If $l_+\\in[1,2]$, then $$\\label{spec:outer-true-det}\n  \\sigma_y(\\bar p/l_+)\\ge c\\sigma_x(p).$$ Comparison <a href=\"#spec:center-true-det\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:center-true-det\">[spec:center-true-det]</a> permits the longer reversed vector $\\bar p/l$ to have a conjugate endpoint.",
      "explanation": "Radial shortening and reversal compare exponential determinants for the center and outer rays in their respective directions.",

      "id": "C012",

      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-radial-determinants",
          "exact_quote": "With a constant depending only on $(M,g)$, if $v\\in G_x$ and $1/2\\le\\theta\\le1$, then $$\\label{spec:radial-det}\n  \\sigma_x(\\theta v)\\ge c\\sigma_x(v).$$ Suppose $y=\\exp_xp$, $p\\in G_x$, and $\\bar p=-P_{x\\to y}p$. If $l\\in[1/2,1)$ and $\\bar p/l\\in G_y$, then $$\\label{spec:center-true-det}\n  \\sigma_y(\\bar p/l)\\le C\\sigma_x(p).$$ If $l_+\\in[1,2]$, then $$\\label{spec:outer-true-det}\n  \\sigma_y(\\bar p/l_+)\\ge c\\sigma_x(p).$$ Comparison <a href=\"#spec:center-true-det\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:center-true-det\">[spec:center-true-det]</a> permits the longer reversed vector $\\bar p/l$ to have a conjugate endpoint.",

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-spec-radial-determinants"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-pullback",
        "exact_quote": "There is $c_{\\rm p}>0$, depending only on $(M,g)$, with the following property. Let $y\\in M$, $r\\in T_yM$, and $1<T_*\\le2$ satisfy $T_*r\\in G_y$. Put $$\\gamma(s)=\\exp_y(sr),\\quad x=\\gamma(1),\\quad\n  p=-P_{y\\to x}r,\\quad L=(d\\exp_x)_p,\n  \\qquad \\tau=1+\\frac{T_*-1}{2}.$$ Then all costs appearing in $$\\label{spec:half-extension-gain}\n  L^*\\left[A_y(r)-\\frac1\\tau A_y(\\tau r)\\right]L\n       \\succeq c_{\\rm p}(T_*-1)\\mathrm{Id}$$ are smooth. If the terminal ray at time $T_*$ is nonconjugate, the same inequality holds with $\\tau$ replaced by $T_*$ and its chosen smooth branch. In particular, for $T_*=1/l$ this latter inequality reads $$\\label{spec:full-extension-gain}\n  L^*\\left[A_y(r)-l A_y(r/l)\\right]L\n       \\succeq c_{\\rm p}(1-l)\\mathrm{Id},\n       \\qquad 1/2\\le l<1.$$ Without any uniformity assertion, the source Hessian of divided action decreases strictly in the positive definite order between any two nonconjugate times on a minimizing ray.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-pullback",
          "exact_quote": "There is $c_{\\rm p}>0$, depending only on $(M,g)$, with the following property. Let $y\\in M$, $r\\in T_yM$, and $1<T_*\\le2$ satisfy $T_*r\\in G_y$. Put $$\\gamma(s)=\\exp_y(sr),\\quad x=\\gamma(1),\\quad\n  p=-P_{y\\to x}r,\\quad L=(d\\exp_x)_p,\n  \\qquad \\tau=1+\\frac{T_*-1}{2}.$$ Then all costs appearing in $$\\label{spec:half-extension-gain}\n  L^*\\left[A_y(r)-\\frac1\\tau A_y(\\tau r)\\right]L\n       \\succeq c_{\\rm p}(T_*-1)\\mathrm{Id}$$ are smooth. If the terminal ray at time $T_*$ is nonconjugate, the same inequality holds with $\\tau$ replaced by $T_*$ and its chosen smooth branch. In particular, for $T_*=1/l$ this latter inequality reads $$\\label{spec:full-extension-gain}\n  L^*\\left[A_y(r)-l A_y(r/l)\\right]L\n       \\succeq c_{\\rm p}(1-l)\\mathrm{Id},\n       \\qquad 1/2\\le l<1.$$ Without any uniformity assertion, the source Hessian of divided action decreases strictly in the positive definite order between any two nonconjugate times on a minimizing ray.",
          "source_id": "PM-360.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is $c_{\\rm p}>0$, depending only on $(M,g)$, with the following property. Let $y\\in M$, $r\\in T_yM$, and $1<T_*\\le2$ satisfy $T_*r\\in G_y$. If the terminal ray at time $T_*$ is nonconjugate, the same inequality holds with $\\tau$ replaced by $T_*$ and its chosen smooth branch. In particular, for $T_*=1/l$ this latter inequality reads $$\\label{spec:full-extension-gain}\n  L^*\\left[A_y(r)-l A_y(r/l)\\right]L\n       \\succeq c_{\\rm p}(1-l)\\mathrm{Id},\n       \\qquad 1/2\\le l<1.$$ Without any uniformity assertion, the source Hessian of divided action decreases strictly in the positive definite order between any two nonconjugate times on a minimizing ray.",
      "explanation": "An extra minimizing extension gives an isotropic gain after pullback, without differentiating a Hessian at a conjugate terminal point.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

          "exact_quote": "There is $c_{\\rm p}>0$, depending only on $(M,g)$, with the following property. Let $y\\in M$, $r\\in T_yM$, and $1<T_*\\le2$ satisfy $T_*r\\in G_y$. Put $$\\gamma(s)=\\exp_y(sr),\\quad x=\\gamma(1),\\quad\n  p=-P_{y\\to x}r,\\quad L=(d\\exp_x)_p,\n  \\qquad \\tau=1+\\frac{T_*-1}{2}.$$ Then all costs appearing in $$\\label{spec:half-extension-gain}\n  L^*\\left[A_y(r)-\\frac1\\tau A_y(\\tau r)\\right]L\n       \\succeq c_{\\rm p}(T_*-1)\\mathrm{Id}$$ are smooth. In particular, for $T_*=1/l$ this latter inequality reads $$\\label{spec:full-extension-gain}\n  L^*\\left[A_y(r)-l A_y(r/l)\\right]L\n       \\succeq c_{\\rm p}(1-l)\\mathrm{Id},\n       \\qquad 1/2\\le l<1.$$ Without any uniformity assertion, the source Hessian of divided action decreases strictly in the positive definite order between any two nonconjugate times on a minimizing ray.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Assume weak MTW. Fix $a_0>0$ and $B<\\infty$. There is $c_{\\rm s}>0$, depending only on $(M,g),a_0,B$, such that the following holds. Let $x\\in M$, $p,q\\in T_xM$, $q\\ne0$, satisfy $$\\label{spec:segment-hypotheses}\n  p+tq\\in G_x\\quad(-a_0\\le t\\le1),\\qquad\n  p\\cdot q>0,\\qquad\n  \\frac{|q|^2}{p\\cdot q}\\le B.$$ Then, for every $1\\le j\\le n$, $$\\label{spec:ordered-comparison}\n  s_j(x,p)\\ge c_{\\rm s}s_j(x,p+q).$$ In particular, $$\\label{spec:product-comparison}\n  \\sigma_x(p)\\ge c_{\\rm s}^n\\sigma_x(p+q).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-outward",
        "exact_quote": "Assume weak MTW and the convexity of $G_x$ established in Corollary <a href=\"#geo:convexity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:convexity\">7</a>. Fix $\\mu\\in(0,1)$ and positive constants $\\kappa,L_0$. Suppose $$p=\\sum_{i=1}^N m_i p_i,\\qquad\n  p_i\\in G_x,\\quad m_i>0,\\quad\\sum_i m_i=1,$$ and for one index $i$ there are $D>0$, a unit vector $e$, and $t_i>0$ such that $$\\label{spec:outward-data}\n  p_i=p+t_i e,\\quad h:=p\\cdot e>0,\\quad\n  h^2\\ge\\kappa D,\\quad\n  |p_i|^2-|p|^2\\le L_0D,\\quad m_i\\ge\\mu.$$ Then $$\\label{spec:outward-det}\n  \\sigma_x(p)\\ge c\\sigma_x(p_i),$$ where $c>0$ depends only on $(M,g),\\mu,\\kappa,L_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-outward",

          "source_id": "PM-360.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-sc-power",
        "exact_quote": "If there are $\\beta>0$, $C_0<\\infty$, and $r_0>0$ such that $F(r)\\leq C_0r^\\beta$ for $0<r<r_0$, then all the optimal maps in the fixed density class have homeomorphic representatives with a common Hölder estimate in both directions.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sc-plateau",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $F$ has no power upper bound near zero. There are $r_j<R_j\\to0$, pairs $(u_j,v_j)\\in\\mathcal C$, poles $x_j$, and numbers $a_j,D_j$ with $D_j>0$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\min_{S_{r_j}(x_j;u_j,v_j)}v_j=a_j,\\qquad\n \\max_{S_{r_j}(x_j;u_j,v_j)}v_j=a_j+D_j,               \\label{sc:endlevels}\\\\\n \\frac{r_j}{R_j}\\longrightarrow0,\\qquad\n F(R_j)\\leq(1+\\delta_j)D_j,\\qquad \\delta_j\\longrightarrow0,\\qquad\n \\frac{|\\log D_j|}{|\\log R_j|}\\longrightarrow0.       \\label{sc:plateau-data}\n\\end{gathered}$$ Consequently $R_j/D_j^k\\to0$ for every fixed $k>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sc-plateau",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $F$ has no power upper bound near zero.           "source_id": "PM-360.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose that $F$ has no power upper bound near zero.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sc-templates",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed smooth function $B_c:\\mathbb R\\to(-1,1)$ with bounded derivatives such that $$B_c'<0\\text{ on }\\mathbb R,\\qquad\n B_c(s)\\geq1-\\varepsilon_0\\quad(s\\leq1/2),\\qquad\n B_c(s)<0\\quad(s\\geq3/4).$$ Given a cap $M_0\\geq2$ and $K\\geq1$, there are $0<\\eta\\leq1/128$ and a smooth bounded function $B_o\\geq0$, with bounded derivatives, satisfying $$\\begin{aligned}\n &B_o(s)\\geq1\\quad(s\\leq1-\\eta),\\qquad\n B_o(1)=0,\\qquad 1\\leq B_o(0)\\leq1+\\varepsilon_0,       \\label{sc:outer-values}\\\\\n &B_o(s)\\geq B_o(0)+1\n       \\quad(1/16\\leq s\\leq1-\\eta),            \\label{sc:barrier}\\\\\n &B_o'(s)>0,\\quad B_o''(s)\\leq-K\n       \\quad\\text{if }-2\\eta\\leq s\\leq1-\\eta\n                      \\text{ and }B_o(s)\\leq M_0.     \\label{sc:curvature}\n\\end{aligned}$$ One can arrange $\\|B_o\\|_\\infty\\leq2M_0+8$, independently of $K$, and make $\\eta$ arbitrarily smaller if desired.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sc-templates",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed smooth function $B_c:\\mathbb R\\to(-1,1)$ with bounded derivatives such that $$B_c'<0\\text{ on }\\mathbb R,\\qquad\n B_c(s)\\geq1-\\varepsilon_0\\quad(s\\leq1/2),\\qquad\n B_c(s)<0\\quad(s\\geq3/4).$$ Given a cap $M_0\\geq2$ and $K\\geq1$, there are $0<\\eta\\leq1/128$ and a smooth bounded function $B_o\\geq0$, with bounded derivatives, satisfying $$\\begin{aligned}\n &B_o(s)\\geq1\\quad(s\\leq1-\\eta),\\qquad\n B_o(1)=0,\\qquad 1\\leq B_o(0)\\leq1+\\varepsilon_0,       \\label{sc:outer-values}\\\\\n &B_o(s)\\geq B_o(0)+1\n       \\quad(1/16\\leq s\\leq1-\\eta),            \\label{sc:barrier}\\\\\n &B_o'(s)>0,\\quad B_o''(s)\\leq-K\n       \\quad\\text{if }-2\\eta\\leq s\\leq1-\\eta\n                      \\text{ and }B_o(s)\\leq M_0.     \\label{sc:curvature}\n\\end{aligned}$$ One can arrange $\\|B_o\\|_\\infty\\leq2M_0+8$, independently of $K$, and make $\\eta$ arbitrarily smaller if desired.",
          "source_id": "PM-360.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a fixed smooth function $B_c:\\mathbb R\\to(-1,1)$ with bounded derivatives such that $$B_c'<0\\text{ on }\\mathbb R,\\qquad\n B_c(s)\\geq1-\\varepsilon_0\\quad(s\\leq1/2),\\qquad\n B_c(s)<0\\quad(s\\geq3/4).$$ Given a cap $M_0\\geq2$ and $K\\geq1$, there are $0<\\eta\\leq1/128$ and a smooth bounded function $B_o\\geq0$, with bounded derivatives, satisfying $$\\begin{aligned}\n &B_o(s)\\geq1\\quad(s\\leq1-\\eta),\\qquad\n B_o(1)=0,\\qquad 1\\leq B_o(0)\\leq1+\\varepsilon_0,       \\label{sc:outer-values}\\\\\n &B_o(s)\\geq B_o(0)+1\n       \\quad(1/16\\leq s\\leq1-\\eta),            \\label{sc:barrier}\\\\\n &B_o'(s)>0,\\quad B_o''(s)\\leq-K\n       \\quad\\text{if }-2\\eta\\leq s\\leq1-\\eta\n                      \\text{ and }B_o(s)\\leq M_0.     \\label{sc:curvature}\n\\end{aligned}$$ One can arrange $\\|B_o\\|_\\infty\\leq2M_0+8$, independently of $K$, and make $\\eta$ arbitrarily smaller if desired.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sc-switch",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently small selected scales, uniformly for $b\\in[b_-,b_+]$, there is $$\\frac b{32D}\\leq\\tau_b\\leq\\frac{8b}D\n \\label{sc:switch-time}$$ such that, at $x_b=\\exp_{x_0}(\\tau_b p_0)$, the function $g_b$ has active endpoints $y_-$ and $y_+$ with $$-2\\eta\\leq s_{y_-}<1/16,\\qquad s_{y_+}\\geq1-\\eta.$$ The original endpoints $y_0,y_1$ and every active endpoint at this pole lie in one original section $S_{C b}(x_b;u,v)$, where $C$ depends only on the geometry and $H_o$. In particular every active level is in $[-\\delta_j,1+\\delta_j]$. For every minimizing log $q_i$ to an active endpoint, $$|q_i|^2=P-2D s_{y_i}+O_{H_o}(\\tau_b+b)\n        =P-2D s_{y_i}+o(D).\n \\label{sc:switch-norms}$$ The constants in <a href=\"#sc:switch-time\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sc:switch-time\">[sc:switch-time]</a> are independent of $K$ and of the height and derivatives of the outer template.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sc-switch",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently small selected scales, uniformly for $b\\in[b_-,b_+]$, there is $$\\frac b{32D}\\leq\\tau_b\\leq\\frac{8b}D\n \\label{sc:switch-time}$$ such that, at $x_b=\\exp_{x_0}(\\tau_b p_0)$, the function $g_b$ has active endpoints $y_-$ and $y_+$ with $$-2\\eta\\leq s_{y_-}<1/16,\\qquad s_{y_+}\\geq1-\\eta.$$ The original endpoints $y_0,y_1$ and every active endpoint at this pole lie in one original section $S_{C b}(x_b;u,v)$, where $C$ depends only on the geometry and $H_o$. In particular every active level is in $[-\\delta_j,1+\\delta_j]$. For every minimizing log $q_i$ to an active endpoint, $$|q_i|^2=P-2D s_{y_i}+O_{H_o}(\\tau_b+b)\n        =P-2D s_{y_i}+o(D).\n \\label{sc:switch-norms}$$ The constants in <a href=\"#sc:switch-time\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sc:switch-time\">[sc:switch-time]</a> are independent of $K$ and of the height and derivatives of the outer template.",
          "source_id": "PM-360.2"
        }

In particular every active level is in $[-\\delta_j,1+\\delta_j]$. For every minimizing log $q_i$ to an active endpoint, $$|q_i|^2=P-2D s_{y_i}+O_{H_o}(\\tau_b+b)\n        =P-2D s_{y_i}+o(D).\n \\label{sc:switch-norms}$$ The constants in <a href=\"#sc:switch-time\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sc:switch-time\">[sc:switch-time]</a> are independent of $K$ and of the height and derivatives of the outer template.",
      "explanation": "Moving the pole toward the low endpoint forces simultaneous low and high active endpoints while excluding middle levels.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sc-switch",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently small selected scales, uniformly for $b\\in[b_-,b_+]$, there is $$\\frac b{32D}\\leq\\tau_b\\leq\\frac{8b}D\n \\label{sc:switch-time}$$ such that, at $x_b=\\exp_{x_0}(\\tau_b p_0)$, the function $g_b$ has active endpoints $y_-$ and $y_+$ with $$-2\\eta\\leq s_{y_-}<1/16,\\qquad s_{y_+}\\geq1-\\eta.$$ The original endpoints $y_0,y_1$ and every active endpoint at this pole lie in one original section $S_{C b}(x_b;u,v)$, where $C$ depends only on the geometry and $H_o$.",
          "source_id": "PM-360.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sc-maximum",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a sufficiently small selected scale. For every $t<1$ sufficiently close to one, the continuous function $$\\Phi_t(b,z)=Q_tg_b(z)+Q_{1-t}w_{c,b}(z)-\\pi(b),\\qquad\n (b,z)\\in[b_-,b_+]\\times M,$$ has a positive global maximum with $b$ in the interior of its interval. Both spatial terms are $C^{1,1}$, and both minimizing points are unique. If $x$ and $y$ are these minimizing points at a maximum, there is $p\\in\\partial g_b(x)\\cap G_x$ such that $$z=\\exp_x(tp),\\qquad y=\\exp_xp,\\qquad\n Q_tg_b(z)+Q_{1-t}w_{c,b}(z)=E_b(y)>0.\n \\label{sc:aligned}$$ Moreover $(x,y)$ is before cut.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sc-parameter",
        "exact_quote": "At any maximum in Lemma <a href=\"#sc:maximum\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sc:maximum\">22</a>, let $$p=\\sum_{i=1}^N m_i p_i,\\qquad m_i>0,\\qquad\\sum_i m_i=1,$$ be any finite representation by minimizing logs active for the displayed representation $g_b=w_{o,b}^c$ at its minimizing pole $x$.         "anchor": "stmt-sc-levels",
        "exact_quote": "At a maximum in Lemma <a href=\"#sc:maximum\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sc:maximum\">22</a>, the center and all active endpoints belong to $S_{(H_o+2)b}(x;u,v)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sc-outward",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $p=\\sum_{i=1}^N m_ip_i$ in an $n$-dimensional Euclidean space, with $N\\leq n+1$, $m_i>0$, and $\\sum_i m_i=1$. Suppose $E=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}\\{p_i-p\\}=\\mathbb Re$, where $|e|=1$, and $q=p\\cdot e>0$. Assume the template and level inequalities <a href=\"#sc:parameter-inequality\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sc:parameter-inequality\">[sc:parameter-inequality]</a>, <a href=\"#sc:level-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sc:level-bounds\">[sc:level-bounds]</a>, and $$\\left|\\ |p_i|^2-|p|^2+2D(s_i-s)\\ \\right|\\leq\\zeta D,\\qquad\n 0\\leq\\delta_j\\leq\\zeta\\leq1/16.$$ There is an outward index $i$ with $p_i=p+d_i e$, $d_i>0$, such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n m_i\\geq m_*:=\\frac{c_1}{12(n+1)},\\qquad\n m_id_i\\geq\\frac{c_1D}{8(n+1)q},\\qquad\n d_i\\leq\\frac{3D}{2q},                         \\label{sc:outward-weight}\\\\\n 0<|p_i|^2-|p|^2\\leq3D,                       \\label{sc:outward-norm}\\\\\n -2\\eta\\leq s_i<1-\\eta,\\qquad B_o(s_i)\\leq1/m_*.\n                                                        \\label{sc:outward-cap}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular the cap $M_0=2/m_*$ can be fixed before $K$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-jet-contact-volume",
        "exact_quote": "Let $u,v$ be the dual potentials for an admissible pair of densities, and let $$\\mathcal C=\\{(x,y):u(x)+c(x,y)+v(y)=0\\}.$$ For a Borel set $A$ on either side, its contact image satisfies $$\\label{jet:contact-volume-eq}\n \\frac{\\lambda}{\\Lambda}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(A)\n \\le \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(\\mathcal C(A))\n \\le \\frac{\\Lambda}{\\lambda}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(A).$$ Projections are interpreted in the completed volume measures.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-jet-contact-volume",
          "exact_quote": "Let $u,v$ be the dual potentials for an admissible pair of densities, and let $$\\mathcal C=\\{(x,y):u(x)+c(x,y)+v(y)=0\\}.$$ For a Borel set $A$ on either side, its contact image satisfies $$\\label{jet:contact-volume-eq}\n \\frac{\\lambda}{\\Lambda}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(A)\n \\le \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(\\mathcal C(A))\n \\le \\frac{\\Lambda}{\\lambda}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(A).$$ Projections are interpreted in the completed volume measures.",
          "source_id": "PM-360.2"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-jet-viscosity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $v$ be one of the original dual potentials. Suppose a $C^2$ function $\\phi$ touches $v$ from below at $y$, its gradient $r=\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}\\phi(y)$ belongs to $I(y)$, and $$W=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}\\phi(y)+A_y(r)>0.$$ Then $$\\label{jet:viscosity-eq}\n \\sigma_y(r)\\det W\\le \\Lambda/\\lambda.$$ The same conclusion holds for a lower Taylor jet with these properties, by approximation of its quadratic part.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-jet-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $z_0$ is a point where $F$ has a second-order expansion and $x(z)$ is differentiable. Write $x=x(z_0)$, $z_0=\\exp_x(tp)$, and choose active logs $$p=\\sum_{j=1}^Nm_jp_j,\\qquad m_j>0,\\quad\n \\sum_jm_j=1,\\qquad N\\le n+1,$$ where $y_j=\\exp_xp_j$ and $g(x)+c(x,y_j)+w_o(y_j)=0$. Set $E=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}\\{p_j-p:1\\le j\\le N\\}$. In the fixed-pole coordinates $z(q)=\\exp_x(tq)$, define $$\\label{jet:L-definition}\n L=D_q^2\\{c_t(x,z(q))-F(z(q))\\}\\big|_{q=p}.$$ Then $$\\label{jet:L-properties}\n L\\ge0,\\qquad LE=0.$$ For every $j$, the function $$\\Psi_j(d)=w_o(\\exp_x(p_j+d))+\\tfrac12|p_j+d|^2$$ has at $d=0$ the lower Taylor jet $(0,m_jL)$, after subtracting its value at zero. If $p_j$ is nonconjugate, this is an endpoint lower test in the smooth chosen cost branch.*\n\n*More precisely, for $t<s<1$ put $$Q_s=\\frac1tA_x(tp)-\\frac1s\\sum_jm_j A_x(sp_j).$$ Then $Q_s\\ge0$ on $E^\\perp$, and $m_j(L+LQ_sL)$ is a lower Taylor Hessian for $\\Psi_j$.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-jet-semibound",
        "exact_quote": "Fix one set of scales, one original potential $v$, and the two templates. Let $t_j\\uparrow1$ and $b_j\\in[b_-,b_+]$. Put $$F_j=Q_{t_j}g_{b_j},\\qquad G_j=Q_{1-t_j}w_{c,b_j}.$$ Let $z_j$ be simultaneous differentiability points for the objects in Lemma <a href=\"#jet:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"jet:transfer\">28</a>, with second-order expansions of $G_j$, whose center levels lie in the fixed compact interval above. Suppose $$|\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}(F_j+G_j)(z_j)|\\longrightarrow0.$$ Let $x_j$ be the prefix pole and $z_j=\\exp_{x_j}(t_jp_j)$. The matrix $H_j$ of <a href=\"#jet:H-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"jet:H-definition\">[jet:H-definition]</a> satisfies $$\\label{jet:semibound-eq}\n H_j\\ge c\\frac{b_j}{D}\\mathrm{Id}\n       -C\\frac{b_j}{D^2}p_jp_j^*-\\varepsilon_j\\mathrm{Id},\n \\qquad \\varepsilon_j\\longrightarrow0.$$ The constants $c,C$ depend on the geometry and the fixed center template, but not on $K$. The errors tend to zero with the prefix and gradient errors at the fixed scales. No upper bound on $H_j$, and no Alexandrov Hessian of $v$ at the center, is assumed.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix $b>0$ and an active outer pair $$g_b(x)+c(x,y)+\\varphi_o(v(y))=0.$$ Choose any minimizing reverse log $r$ from $y$ to $x$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-jet-outer-test",
        "exact_quote": "At a pair as in Lemma <a href=\"#jet:outer-conjugacy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"jet:outer-conjugacy\">30</a>, let $p=\\log_xy$ be the corresponding forward log, $B=(d\\exp_x)_p$, and let $H_o\\ge0$ be a lower Taylor Hessian, with zero linear part, of $d\\mapsto w_o(\\exp_x(p+d))+\\tfrac12|p+d|^2$. Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n V_o={}&H_o+B^*\\{lA_y(r/l)-A_y(r)\\}B\n             -\\frac{\\varphi_o''(v(y))}{l^2}pp^* .\n \\label{jet:outer-form}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Then $V_o>0$ and $$\\label{jet:outer-det}\n \\det V_o\\le\n l^n(\\Lambda/\\lambda)\\,\n       \\frac{\\sigma_x(p)^2}{\\sigma_y(r/l)}.$$ The middle term in <a href=\"#jet:outer-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"jet:outer-form\">[jet:outer-form]</a> is positive definite. On a compact family of active pairs where $b>0$ is bounded away from zero, $\\varphi_o'>1$ and $\\varphi_o''<0$ hold with fixed strict margins, this term has a uniform positive definite baseline and the chosen exponential branches are uniformly nonsingular.",
        "source_id": "PM-360.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "At a pair as in Lemma <a href=\"#jet:outer-conjugacy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"jet:outer-conjugacy\">30</a>, let $p=\\log_xy$ be the corresponding forward log, $B=(d\\exp_x)_p$, and let $H_o\\ge0$ be a lower Taylor Hessian, with zero linear part, of $d\\mapsto w_o(\\exp_x(p+d))+\\tfrac12|p+d|^2$. Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n V_o={}&H_o+B^*\\{lA_y(r/l)-A_y(r)\\}B\n             -\\frac{\\varphi_o''(v(y))}{l^2}pp^* .\n \\label{jet:outer-form}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Then $V_o>0$ and $$\\label{jet:outer-det}\n \\det V_o\\le\n l^n(\\Lambda/\\lambda)\\,\n       \\frac{\\sigma_x(p)^2}{\\sigma_y(r/l)}.$$ The middle term in <a href=\"#jet:outer-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"jet:outer-form\">[jet:outer-form]</a> is positive definite. On a compact family of active pairs where $b>0$ is bounded away from zero, $\\varphi_o'>1$ and $\\varphi_o''<0$ hold with fixed strict margins, this term has a uniform positive definite baseline and the chosen exponential branches are uniformly nonsingular.",
          "source_id": "PM-360.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-jet-outer-test"
    },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Squared-distance cost and open injectivity domain** ([source](#eq:weak-mtw)): Write $d$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}$ for its distance and volume measure, and set $$c(x,y)=\frac12d(x,y)^2,\qquad I(x)=\{p\in T_xM:\ d(x,\exp_x(ap))=a\|p\| \text{ for some }a>1\}.$$
- **Weak Ma–Trudinger–Wang condition** ([source](#eq:weak-mtw)): We assume the weak Ma–Trudinger–Wang condition: for every $p\in I(x)$ and $\xi,\eta\in T_xM$ with $\xi\perp\eta$, $$\label{eq:weak-mtw} -\frac32\left.\frac{\partial^4}{\partial s^2\partial t^2} c\bigl(\exp_x(t\xi),\exp_x(p+s\eta)\bigr)\right\|_{s=t=0}\geq0.$$
- **Optimal map** ([source](#eq:weak-mtw)): For probability densities $\rho_0,\rho_1$, an optimal map is a measurable map $T$ with $T_\#(\rho_0\mathop{\mathrm{vol}})=\rho_1\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}$ that minimizes $\int_M c(x,T(x))\rho_0(x)\,d\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(x)$ among maps with that pushforward.
- **Dual potentials** ([source](#pm-h-4)): We use dual potentials with $$u(x)=\sup_y\{-c(x,y)-v(y)\},\qquad v(y)=\sup_x\{-c(x,y)-u(x)\}.$$
- **Ordinary subdifferential** ([source](#pm-h-4)): The symbol $\partial u(x)$ denotes the ordinary subdifferential of the semiconvex function, with covectors identified with tangent vectors by the metric.
- **Closed injectivity domain and intermediate operators** ([source](#pm-h-4)): We write $$G_x=\{p\in T_xM:d(x,\exp_xp)=\|p\|\}=\overline{I(x)},\qquad c_t=c/t,\qquad Q_tf(z)=\min_x\{f(x)+c_t(x,z)\},$$ and, for $p\in I(x)$, $$A_x(p)=D^2_{xx}c(x,\exp_xp),\qquad \sigma_x(p)=\|\det(d\exp_x)_p\|.$$
- **Potential** ([source](#sec:geometry)): A potential is a function $u(x)=\max_y\{-c(x,y)-w(y)\}$ with a continuous datum $w$; replacing $w$ by the cost transform of $u$ gives a dual pair.
- **Lifted gap section** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a dual pair $(u,v)$, the lifted gap section at $x$ and height $r\geq0$ is $$\mathcal S_x(r)=\left\{p\in G_x: u(x)+\frac{\|p\|^2}{2}+v(\exp_xp)\leq r\right\}.$$
- **Active minimizing logs** ([source](#stmt-geo-prelim)): For a continuous representing datum $w$, define $$\mathcal V_u(x)=\{p\in G_x: u(x)+c(x,\exp_xp)+w(\exp_xp)=0\}.$$
- **Endpoint sections and class-wide oscillation** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For $(u,v)\in\mathcal C$ and $r\geq0$, put $$S_r(x;u,v)=\{y\in M:u(x)+c(x,y)+v(y)\leq r\}, \qquad F(r)=\sup_{(u,v)\in\mathcal C,\ x\in M} \mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{S_r(x;u,v)}v.$$
- **Lower Taylor jet** ([source](#sec:jets)): Throughout this section, a *lower Taylor jet* $(\zeta,B)$ of a function $f$ at the origin means $$f(h)\ge f(0)+\zeta\cdot h+\tfrac12 B(h,h)+o(\|h\|^2).$$

Assume the template and level inequalities <a href=\"#sc:parameter-inequality\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sc:parameter-inequality\">[sc:parameter-inequality]</a>, <a href=\"#sc:level-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sc:level-bounds\">[sc:level-bounds]</a>, and $$\\left|\\ |p_i|^2-|p|^2+2D(s_i-s)\\ \\right|\\leq\\zeta D,\\qquad\n 0\\leq\\delta_j\\leq\\zeta\\leq1/16.$$ There is an outward index $i$ with $p_i=p+d_i e$, $d_i>0$, such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n m_i\\geq m_*:=\\frac{c_1}{12(n+1)},\\qquad\n m_id_i\\geq\\frac{c_1D}{8(n+1)q},\\qquad\n d_i\\leq\\frac{3D}{2q},                         \\label{sc:outward-weight}\\\\\n 0<|p_i|^2-|p|^2\\leq3D,                       \\label{sc:outward-norm}\\\\\n -2\\eta\\leq s_i<1-\\eta,\\qquad B_o(s_i)\\leq1/m_*.\n                                                        \\label{sc:outward-cap}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular the cap $M_0=2/m_*$ can be fixed before $K$. All these constants are independent of $K$.",
            "source_id": "PM-360.2"
          }
        ],

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-geo-support) / [proof](#pm-h-5): Density-free global support and intermediate regularization -> Convex lifted sections and controlled intermediate poles
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-spec-comparison) / [proof](#pm-h-5): Jacobi factorization and spectral comparison -> Uniform Jacobian comparisons
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-sc-plateau) / [proof](#pm-h-5): Contradiction using separated gap scales -> An almost constant oscillation plateau
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-sc-parameter) / [proof](#pm-h-5): Scalar modifications, regularized maxima, and amplitude variation -> A universal active barycentric parameter inequality
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-jet-determinants) / [proof](#sec:jets): Endpoint lower jets and ordered limiting arguments -> Nonconjugate outer branch and bounded center Hessian
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-jet-matrix) / [proof](#sec:jets): Nonsingular center sampling and determinant gain -> Positive center and outer forms with determinant gain
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-jet-contradiction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-contradiction): Compare determinant gain with fixed geometric Jacobian bounds -> A common positive power bound
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:conclusion): Convert section oscillation into endpoint uniqueness and Hölder control -> Homeomorphic transport representatives with a common estimate in both directions

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **transport_class**: The optimal maps have homeomorphic representatives with common Hölder estimates in both directions for the fixed admissible density class.
- **intermediate_projection**: The subgradient-graph projection is a homeomorphism at intermediate times; arbitrary potentials receive no time-one injectivity guarantee.
- **density_correspondence**: Contact-volume and density determinant bounds concern the original dual potentials; modified envelopes do not inherit density bounds.
- **smooth_curvature**: Weak MTW is imposed where the squared-distance cost is smooth, while the transport theorem permits conjugate cut points.
- **nonconstructive_scope**: The positive exponent is obtained by contradiction and compactness, with no explicit exponent or uniform estimate across varying metrics or density bounds.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **explicit_exponent**: Use the proof as a formula for the common Hölder exponent. -> An existence argument is treated as an explicit quantitative computation.
- **vary_geometry**: Reuse the same Hölder constants after changing the metric or density bounds. -> Class uniformity is extended beyond its fixed parameters.
- **time_one**: Invert the endpoint projection for an arbitrary cost potential. -> Multiple poles can share an endpoint.
- **modified_density**: Apply the original density Jacobian bound directly to a scalar-modified potential or envelope. -> A density bound is transferred to a correspondence for which it is not claimed.
- **discrete_transport**: Treat a discrete transport plan as a homeomorphic map certified by the theorem. -> Discrete marginals do not establish the measurable positive-density class on the fixed manifold.
- **cut_hessian**: Evaluate the smooth-cost curvature inequality directly at a nonsmooth cut pair. -> An undefined smooth Hessian is used as curvature evidence.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The common transport exponent is obtained by contradiction and compactness; the paper gives no explicit exponent. Its finite matrix comparisons do not compute the common Hölder estimate for the full density class.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L001** (hypothesis): The transport estimate requires the stated smooth connected compact boundaryless Riemannian geometry, dimension regime, squared-distance cost, and weak MTW condition. Blocked use: ['Applying the estimate to a different cost or a geometry outside the stated manifold regime.']
- **L002** (hypothesis): The density class must be nonempty and consist of measurable probability densities with common positive lower and finite upper bounds. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem directly to atomic marginals or densities that vanish.', 'Omitting probability normalization or changing the fixed density bounds.']
- **L003** (nonconstructive): The proof establishes a positive exponent by contradiction and compactness and supplies no explicit exponent. Blocked use: ['Reporting a computed Hölder exponent as supplied by the theorem.', 'Treating the proof as a numerical transport solver with a certified approximation error.']
- **L004** (hypothesis): The estimate is common to the fixed density class; uniformity over varying metrics or density bounds is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Claiming common constants across varying metrics or unrestricted density classes.']
- **L005** (counterexample_scope): Arbitrary cost potentials need not have injective endpoint projections; the stated inverse projections and regularizations use intermediate times. Blocked use: ['Inferring an inverse endpoint projection for every cost potential.']
- **L006** (edge_case): The theorem allows conjugate cut points; no smooth cost Hessian at such a pair may be inferred from the curvature hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Differentiating a nonsmooth global cost at a conjugate cut endpoint without the stated support or branch argument.']
- **L007** (hypothesis): The inverse transport representative is obtained by reversing the positive-density pair. Blocked use: ['Claiming inverse transport regularity after dropping the hypotheses on the reversed pair.']
- **L008** (edge_case): Density and Jacobian bounds for the original contact correspondence do not automatically apply to modified potentials or their envelopes. Blocked use: ['Using the original density equation for a modified potential without the endpoint and lower-test argument.']
- **L009** (approximation): A transfer of the continuum regularity estimate to a discrete or regularized computation requires a separate approximation justification. Blocked use: ['Treating a discrete computed plan as certified by the continuum homeomorphism theorem.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 360 (`lean/docs/360.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Global Support and Convex Injectivity Domains under Weak MTW](../../preprints/Global-Support-and-Convex-Injectivity-Domains-under-Weak-MTW-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Uniform Bi-Hölder Transport from Weak MTW](../../preprints/Uniform-Bi-Holder-Transport-from-Weak-MTW-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> On a compact connected smooth Riemannian manifold of dimension at least two, the formalized result proves that weak MTW curvature implies convexity of every open tangent injectivity domain. Prior convexity or nonfocality is not assumed. Additional formalized results give global support at ordinary subgradients, compact convex lifted gap sections with the stated diameter bound, and scale and local-injectivity estimates for finite target families. The latter finite-family result does not cover the paper's arbitrary-potential local-injectivity assertion.
>
> The formalized result gives uniform bi-Hölder optimal transport for squared-distance cost on each fixed compact connected smooth Riemannian manifold of dimension at least two satisfying weak MTW. For probability densities between fixed positive lower and finite upper bounds, one exponent and constant work for every pair of densities. The optimal map is a homeomorphism, is unique almost everywhere, and both it and its inverse satisfy the global Hölder bound. The constants are uniform over the density class on the fixed manifold, not over varying metrics.

Comparator source `BiholderTransport.lean` (5413 bytes, sha256 `0a01595c5b2289901b195b6a358e5c463146d6e1f3a106250139672bed2ff20e`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open Set MeasureTheory Manifold Bundle
open scoped ContDiff Manifold ENNReal NNReal Topology

namespace WeakMTWTransport

abbrev Model (n : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n)

section MetricDefinitions

variable {M : Type*} [MetricSpace M]

/-- Squared-distance cost with factor one half. -/
def cost (x y : M) : ℝ := dist x y ^ 2 / 2

/-- Calibration of Hausdorff measure to Euclidean volume. -/
def euclideanVolumeFactor (n : ℕ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  volume (Metric.ball (0 : Model n) 1) /
    (Measure.hausdorffMeasure (n : ℝ) : Measure (Model n))
      (Metric.ball (0 : Model n) 1)

variable [MeasurableSpace M]

/-- Riemannian volume calibrated to Lebesgue measure in Euclidean space. -/
def metricVolume [BorelSpace M] (n : ℕ) : Measure M :=
  euclideanVolumeFactor n • Measure.hausdorffMeasure (n : ℝ)

/-- Almost-everywhere measurable probability densities between fixed bounds. -/
def AdmissibleDensity (vol : Measure M) (lam cap : ℝ) (rho : M → ℝ) : Prop :=
  AEMeasurable rho vol ∧ Integrable rho vol ∧
  (∫ x, rho x ∂vol) = 1 ∧ (∀ᵐ x ∂vol, lam ≤ rho x ∧ rho x ≤ cap)

/-- Multiplication by a density, ignoring exceptional negative values on null sets. -/
def densityMeasure (vol : Measure M) (rho : M → ℝ) : Measure M :=
  vol.withDensity (fun x => ENNReal.ofReal (rho x))

/-- Optimality for the Monge minimization problem. -/
def IsOptimalMap (vol : Measure M) (rho0 rho1 : M → ℝ) (T : M → M) : Prop :=
  Measurable T ∧ Measure.map T (densityMeasure vol rho0) = densityMeasure vol rho1 ∧
  ∀ S : M → M, Measurable S →
    Measure.map S (densityMeasure vol rho0) = densityMeasure vol rho1 →
    (∫ x, cost x (T x) * rho0 x ∂vol) ≤
      (∫ x, cost x (S x) * rho0 x ∂vol)

/-- The two global estimates have the very same exponent and constant. -/
def BiHolderEstimate (alpha C : ℝ) (T : M ≃ₜ M) : Prop :=
  (∀ x x', dist (T x) (T x') ≤ C * (dist x x') ^ alpha) ∧
  (∀ y y', dist (T.symm y) (T.symm y') ≤ C * (dist y y') ^ alpha)

end MetricDefinitions

section RiemannianDefinitions

variable {n : ℕ} {M : Type*} [MetricSpace M] [ChartedSpace (Model n) M]
  [IsManifold 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) ∞ M]
  [RiemannianBundle (fun x : M => TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) x)]

/-- Complete affinely parametrized geodesic with prescribed initial tangent.
Local metric minimization is an intrinsic definition of Riemannian geodesics. -/
def IsGeodesicWithInitialData (x : M) (v : TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) x)
    (gamma : ℝ → M) : Prop :=
  ContMDiff 𝓘(ℝ, ℝ) 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) ∞ gamma ∧
  (⟨gamma 0, mfderiv 𝓘(ℝ, ℝ) 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) gamma 0 (1 : ℝ)⟩ :
    TangentBundle 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) M) = ⟨x, v⟩ ∧
  ∀ t : ℝ, ∃ eps : ℝ, 0 < eps ∧
    ∀ s u : ℝ, |s - t| < eps → |u - t| < eps →
      dist (gamma s) (gamma u) = |s - u| * ‖v‖

/-- Evaluation at time one of the geodesic determined by initial data. -/
def riemannianExp (x : M) (v : TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) x) : M := by
  classical
  exact if h : ∃ gamma, IsGeodesicWithInitialData x v gamma then (Classical.choose h) 1 else x

/-- The open injectivity domain, defined by extendible minimizing vectors. -/
def injectivityDomain (x : M) : Set (TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) x) :=
  {p | ∃ a : ℝ, 1 < a ∧ dist x (riemannianExp x (a • p)) = a * ‖p‖}

/-- The Ma–Trudinger–Wang fourth derivative with factor -3/2. -/
def mtw (x : M) (p xi eta : TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) x) : ℝ :=
  -(3 / 2 : ℝ) *
    iteratedDeriv 2 (fun s : ℝ =>
      iteratedDeriv 2 (fun t : ℝ =>
        cost (riemannianExp x (t • xi)) (riemannianExp x (p + s • eta))) 0) 0

/-- Weak MTW only at interior velocities and only for orthogonal test vectors. -/
def WeakMTW : Prop :=
  ∀ (x : M) (p : TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) x), p ∈ injectivityDomain x →
    ∀ xi eta : TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) x,
      inner ℝ xi eta = 0 → 0 ≤ mtw x p xi eta

end RiemannianDefinitions

/-- Uniform bi-Hölder optimal transport for all densities between fixed bounds
on a compact connected smooth Riemannian manifold satisfying weak MTW. -/
theorem uniform_biHolder_transport
    {n : ℕ} (hn : 2 ≤ n) {M : Type*}
    [MetricSpace M] [CompactSpace M] [ConnectedSpace M]
    [ChartedSpace (Model n) M] [IsManifold 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) ∞ M]
    [RiemannianBundle (fun x : M => TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) x)]
    [IsContMDiffRiemannianBundle 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) ∞ (Model n)
      (fun x : M => TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) x)]
    [IsRiemannianManifold 𝓘(ℝ, Model n) M]
    [MeasurableSpace M] [BorelSpace M]
    (hmtw : WeakMTW (n := n) (M := M))
    (lam cap : ℝ) (hlam : 0 < lam) (hcap : lam ≤ cap)
    (hclass : ∃ rho : M → ℝ, AdmissibleDensity (metricVolume n) lam cap rho) :
    ∃ alpha C : ℝ, 0 < alpha ∧ alpha ≤ 1 ∧ 0 ≤ C ∧
      ∀ rho0 rho1 : M → ℝ,
        AdmissibleDensity (metricVolume n) lam cap rho0 →
        AdmissibleDensity (metricVolume n) lam cap rho1 →
        ∃ T : M ≃ₜ M,
          IsOptimalMap (metricVolume n) rho0 rho1 T ∧
          (∀ S : M → M, IsOptimalMap (metricVolume n) rho0 rho1 S →
            S =ᵐ[densityMeasure (metricVolume n) rho0] T) ∧
          BiHolderEstimate alpha C T := by
  sorry

end WeakMTWTransport

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the weak Ma–Trudinger–Wang condition on a fixed smooth connected compact boundaryless Riemannian manifold of dimension at least two implies a common Hölder estimate for optimal transport maps and their inverses over the entire class of probability densities with fixed positive upper and lower bounds. The maps have homeomorphic representatives, conjugate cut points are allowed, and no density regularity is assumed.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For the squared-distance cost on a compact Riemannian manifold, positive upper and lower density bounds give an almost-everywhere optimal map, but continuity depends on the geometry of the cost. The weak Ma–Trudinger–Wang condition controls that geometry where the cost is smooth. We prove that it gives a common Hölder estimate for the map and its inverse throughout each fixed density-bounded class, even when transport graphs approach conjugate pairs.

<a id="eq:weak-mtw"></a>
Let $(M,g)$ be a smooth connected compact Riemannian manifold without boundary, of dimension $n\geq2$. Write $d$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}$ for its distance and volume measure, and set $$c(x,y)=\frac12d(x,y)^2,\qquad
 I(x)=\{p\in T_xM:\ d(x,\exp_x(ap))=a|p|
                         \text{ for some }a>1\}.$$ We assume the weak Ma–Trudinger–Wang condition: for every $p\in I(x)$ and $\xi,\eta\in T_xM$ with $\xi\perp\eta$, $$\label{eq:weak-mtw}
 -\frac32\left.\frac{\partial^4}{\partial s^2\partial t^2}
 c\bigl(\exp_x(t\xi),\exp_x(p+s\eta)\bigr)\right|_{s=t=0}\geq0.$$ The cost is smooth near the pair in this formula. No curvature inequality at a nonsmooth cost pair is assumed. For probability densities $\rho_0,\rho_1$, an optimal map is a measurable map $T$ with $T_\#(\rho_0\mathop{\mathrm{vol}})=\rho_1\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}$ that minimizes $\int_M c(x,T(x))\rho_0(x)\,d\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(x)$ among maps with that pushforward.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Uniform bi-Hölder transport). *Fix $(M,g)$ as above and $0<\lambda\leq\Lambda<\infty$ for which the following density class is nonempty. There are $\alpha\in(0,1]$ and $C<\infty$, depending only on $(M,g),\lambda,\Lambda$, such that the following holds for every pair of measurable densities satisfying $$\int_M\rho_i\,d\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}=1,\qquad
